除此处提及的二百九十九幅大型完成画作之外，韦斯特先生还绘有约一百幅肖像画，以及两百余幅钢笔画；后者就构思的崇高境界而言，堪列于其最杰出的杰作之林。因此其全部画作总计超过六百幅。其中若干画作在尺寸上甚至超过了法兰西国家画廊所藏任何名画，且全凭他一人操刀、未受任何其他画师襄助。

韦斯特先生现年约六十八岁。他在天才创造的活跃度上，抑或在才智的勤勉运用上，皆未见衰退之象。自上述目录编就以来，他又创作了数幅杰作，其中三幅我在其画室中格外瞩目：《托比特与多俾亚持鱼》、《亚伯拉罕遣散夏甲与其幼子》、《阿喀琉斯自忒提斯领受崭新铠甲》；传闻他近来绘制了《纳尔逊之死》。他或尚能创作出更多传世力作；尽管据推测在作品数量与多样性上，除鲁本斯外他早已超越了所有其他历史画大师。至于其画作的艺术造诣，我不敢妄加断言以指引他人。除拉斐尔外，在为我们所知的画家中，他无疑堪称最典范的正统大家。

===Original===

The critics find fault with the coloring of Mr. West. But in his works, as in those of Raphael, we do not look for coloring. It is dignity of character, fine expression, delicate design, correct drawing and beautiful disposition of drapery which fix the suffrage of the real judge. All which qualities can only spring from an elevated mind. ↩︎

-

O sanctas gentes, quibus haec nascuntur in hortis Numina.

> *Juv. Sat. 15*.

↩︎

-

The state of the arts and sciences among the ancients, viewed with reference to the event of universal civilization, was faulty in two respects. First, in their comparative estimation: Second, in their flourishing only in one nation at a time. These circumstances might be favorable to the exertions of individual genius; and they may be assigned both as causes of the universal destruction of the arts and sciences by the Gothic conquest, and as reasons why we should not greatly lament that destruction.

From the political state of mankind in the days of their ancient splendor it was natural that those arts which depend on the imagination, such as Architecture, Statuary, Painting, Eloquence and Poetry, should claim the highest rank in the estimation of a people. In several, perhaps all of these, the ancients remain unrivalled. But these are not the arts which tend the most to the general improvement of society. A man in those days would have rendered more service to the world by ascertaining the true figure and movements of the earth, than by originating a heaven and filling it with all the gods of Homer; and had the expenses of the Egyptian pyramids been employed in furnishing ships of discovery and sending them out of the Mediterranean, the nations called civilized would not have been afterwards overrun by Barbarians.

But the sciences of Geography, Navigation and Commerce, with their consequent improvements in Natural Philosophy and Humanity, could not, from the nature of things at that time, become objects of great encouragement or enterprise. Talent was therefore confined to the cultivation of arts more striking to the senses. As these arts were adapted to gratify the vanity of princes, to help carry on the sacred frauds of priests, to fire the ambition of heroes, or to gain causes in popular assemblies, they were brought to a degree of perfection which prevented their being relished or understood by barbarous neighbors.

===Chinese===

批评家对韦斯特先生的着色颇有微词。但在其作品中，正如在拉斐尔的作品中一样，我们并不以着色为首要苛求。正是品格的高贵、精妙的表情、雅致的构图、精准的线条以及衣褶的优美排布，决定了真正鉴赏家的赞许裁决。而所有这些品格唯能孕育自崇高的胸襟。 ↩︎

-

O sanctas gentes, quibus haec nascuntur in hortis Numina.

> 尤维纳利斯《讽刺诗集》第15首。

↩︎

-

古代社会的艺术与科学水准，若以普世文明化这一历史宏旨来审视，在两方面存有缺陷。其一，在相互比勘的估价上；其二，在某一时期唯有单一民族得以繁盛上。这些情势或许利于个体天才的发挥；它们既可被视作哥特人征服导致艺术与科学普遍倾覆的原由，亦可作为我们无需对那场毁灭过分扼腕痛惜的缘由。

从人类古代极盛时期的政治状态来看，那些依托想象力的艺术（诸如建筑、雕塑、绘画、修辞演说与诗歌）在公众估价中高居首位实属自然。在其中数项（或许所有各项）之中，古人至今仍无可匹敌。但这些绝非最能推动社会普世精进的技艺。在那个时代，一个人若能探明大地的真正形态与运转规律，对世界所作出的贡献，远胜于凭空虚构一个天国并以荷马诸神充塞其间；而设若当年修建埃及金字塔的庞大资财能用来装备探险船只并派往地中海之外，所谓文明之国度其后亦不致遭野蛮人肆意蹂躏。

然而地理学、航海学与商贸之学，以及由此促成在自然哲学与人道主义上的精进，受限于彼时的客观情势，断不可能成为重大提倡或进取的对象。因此才智局限于研求对感官刺激更强烈的技艺。因这些技艺适于满足君王的虚荣、协助祭司推行神圣骗局、点燃英雄的野心、或在民众集会中赢得诉讼，它们便被推向了极高的造诣，以至周遭野蛮邻邦根本无法领略或理解。

===Original===

The improvements of the world therefore, whether in literature, sciences or arts, descended with the line of conquest from one nation to another, till the whole were concentred in the Roman empire. Their tendency there was to inspire a contempt for nations less civilized, and to teach the Romans to consider all mankind as the proper objects of their military despotism. These circumstances prepared, through a course of ages, and finally opened a scene of wretchedness at which the human mind has been taught to shudder. But some such convulsion seemed necessary to reduce the nations to a position capable of commencing regular improvements. And, however novel the sentiment may appear, I will venture to say that, as to the prospect of universal civilization, mankind were in a better situation in the time of Charlemagne than they were in the days of Augustus.

The final destruction of the Roman empire left the nations of Europe in circumstances similar to each other; and their consequent rivalship prevented any disproportionate refinement from appearing in any particular region. The principles of government, firmly rooted in the Feudal System, unsocial and unphilosophical as they were, laid the foundation of that balance of power which discourages the Caesars and Alexanders of modern ages from attempting the conquest of the world.

It seems necessary that the arrangement of events in civilizing the world should be in the following order: *first*, all parts of it must be considerably peopled; *second*, the different nations must be known to each other; *third*, their wants must be increased, in order to inspire a passion for commerce. The first of these objects was not probably accomplished till a late period. The second for three centuries past has been greatly accelerated. The third is a necessary consequence of the two former. The spirit of commerce is happily calculated to open an amicable intercourse between all countries, to soften the horrors of war, to enlarge the field of science and to assimilate the manners, feelings and languages of all nations. This leading principle, in its remoter consequences, will produce advantages in favor of free government, give patriotism the character of philanthropy, induce all men to regard each other as brethren and friends and teach them the benefits of peace and harmony among the nations.

===Chinese===

因而世间的进步（无论是文学、科学抑或艺术）皆伴随着征服的脉络从一国传递至另一国，直至全部汇聚于罗马帝国。其在那里的倾向是激起对不甚文明民族的蔑视，并教导罗马人将全人类视作其军事专制的理当目标。这些情势历经数代酝酿，最终拉开了一幕令人类心智为之战栗的悲惨惨景。但某种此类剧变似乎势所必然，方能将万邦归置于能够开启正规精进的地位。且无论此观点看起来多么标新立异，我敢断言：就普世文明的前景而言，查理曼时代的人类境遇远胜于奥古斯都时代。

罗马帝国的最终瓦解使欧洲各邦置身于彼此相似的境遇之中；由此产生的竞争制约了任何特定区域出现畸形的孤立繁荣。牢固植根于封建体系的治理原则——尽管粗鄙且不合哲理——奠定了均势的根基，从而遏制了近代的恺撒与亚历山大们企图征服世界的野心。

在教化世界的进程中，历史事件的序列似乎必须遵循如下法则：其一，大地各部必须繁衍相当的人口；其二，万邦各族必须彼此相知相识；其三，其需求必须相应增长，以激发起对商贸的热望。首项目标直至较晚时代方告达成。第二项目标在过去三个世纪中得到了极大的提速。第三项则是前两者的必然产物。商贸精神极其巧妙地旨在开拓万国之间的友好往来、抚慰战争的酷烈、拓展科学的疆域，并同化各民族的风尚、情愫与语言。这一主导原则在其更为深远的影响中，必将孕育出利于自由政体的丰硕成果，赋予爱国主义以博爱宽宏的品格，促使世人视彼此为骨肉同胞并从普遍幸福中寻求自身的安全。 ↩︎

===Original===

I conceive it no objection to this theory that the progress has hitherto been slow; when we consider the magnitude of the object, the obstructions that were to be removed, and the length of time taken to accomplish it. The future progress will probably be more rapid than the present. Since the invention of printing, the application of the properties of the magnet and the knowledge of the structure of the solar system, it is difficult to conceive of a cause that can produce a new state of barbarism; unless it be some great convulsion in the physical world, so extensive as to change the face of the earth or a considerable part of it. This indeed may have been the case already more than once, since the earth was first peopled with men and antecedent to our histories. But such events have nothing to do with the present argument. ↩︎

-

The planet discovered by Herschel was called by him Georgium Sidus; but in all countries except England it is named Herschel and probably will be so named there after his death and that of the patron to whom his gratitude led him to make this extraordinary dedication.

I would observe that, besides the impropriety of giving it another name than that of the discoverer, it is inconvenient to use a double name or a name composed of two words. Let it be either George or Herschel.

The passage referred to in this note was written before the discovery of the three other planets which are now added to our catalogue. Could my voice have weight in deciding on the names to be given to these new children of the sun, I would call them by the names of their respective discoverers, Piazzi, Olbers and Harding, instead of the senseless and absurd appellations of Ceres, Pallas and Juno. The former method would at least assist us in preserving the history of science; the latter will only tend farther to confuse a very ancient mythology which is already extremely confused, and increase the difficulty of following the faint traces of real knowledge that seems couched under the mass of that mythology; traces which may one day lead to many useful truths in philosophy and morals. ↩︎

-

===Chinese===

当我们考虑到目标的宏伟、有待排除的险阻以及达成目标所耗费的时间跨度，我认为迄今进展之迟缓丝毫不构成对此学说的否定。未来的进步极可能比当下更为迅疾。自从活字印刷的发明、磁石特性的应用以及对太阳系结构的认知确立以来，已很难设想有任何原由足以导致重新陷入野蛮状态；除非是物质世界发生某种巨大的剧烈变动，其广袤足以彻底重塑地表或其相当一部分。在人类最初繁衍且早于我们信史的远古，此类浩劫诚然可能已不止发生过一次。但此类变故同当下的论题毫无干系。 ↩︎

-

赫歇尔所发现的行星曾被他命名为乔治之星；但在除英格兰之外的所有国度，它皆被定名为赫歇尔星，且在他本人及那位因其感恩戴德而施以如此非同寻常奉献的庇护者逝世后，在英国当地恐亦将如此定名。

我谨指出：除不宜赋予发现者之外的名号外，使用双重名称或由两个词构成的名称亦多有不便。莫如索性称其为乔治星，抑或赫歇尔星。

本条注释所指段落草于另外三颗如今已载入我们目录的行星发现之前。若我的意见在决定赋予这些太阳崭新子嗣何种名号上有分毫分量，我必以其各自发现者的名号称呼它们——皮亚齐星、奥尔伯斯星与哈丁星，而非谷神星、智神星与婚神星等空洞荒谬的称号。前者至少有助于我们保留科学的历史；后者只会进一步扰乱早已极度混乱的古老神话，并加剧探寻那似乎掩盖在庞杂神话之下的真实知识微弱踪迹的艰难；而那些踪迹有朝一日或能引领我们通向哲学与道德上的诸多致用真理。 ↩︎

-

===Original===

A most useful book might be written on this subject. It should be a Review of Poets and Historians, as to the moral and political tendency of their works. It should likewise treat of the importance of the task assigned to these two classes of writers. It might attempt to point out the true object they ought to have in view; perhaps do this with such clearness and energy as to gain the attention of writers as well as readers, and thus serve in some measure as a guide to future historians and poets. At least it would prove a guide to readers; and by teaching them how to judge, and what to praise or blame in the accounts of human actions, whether real or fictitious, the public taste would be reformed by degrees. In this case the recorders of heroic actions, as well as the authors of them, would find it necessary to follow this reform, or they must necessarily fail of obtaining the celebrity to which they all aspire.

I think every person who will give himself the trouble to form an opinion on the manner in which actions, called heroic, have been recorded, must find it faulty; and must lament, as one of the misfortunes of society, that writers of these two classes almost universally, from Homer down to Gibbon, have led astray the moral sense of man. In this view we may say in general of poets and historians, as we do of their heroes, that they have injured the cause of humanity almost in proportion to the fame they have acquired.

I would not be understood by this observation to mean that such writers have done no good. Even the works of Homer, which have caused more mischief to mankind than those of any other, have likewise been a fruitful source of a certain species of benefits. They elevate the mind of every reader; they have called forth great exertions of genius in poets, artists, philosophers and heroes, through a long succession of ages. But it remains to be considered what a fruitful source they have likewise been of those false notions of honor and erroneous systems of policy which have governed the actions of men from his day to ours.

===Chinese===

针对这一命题本可撰写一部极富教益的专著。该书当为一部《诗人与历史学家述评》，专注于其著述在道德与政治上的旨归导向。它亦当论及赋予这两类著述者的使命有多么重大。它当致力于指出他们理应怀抱的真正宗旨；并以足够的澄澈与力量博得著述者与读者的双重瞩目，从而在某种程度上充当未来史家与诗人的指引向导。至少它将成为读者的指引；通过教会他们如何在关涉人类行径的真实或虚构记述中进行明断、该褒扬何物、该贬抑何事，公众的品味便将逐步得以矫正。在此情形下，英雄行径的记录者与践行者必会发现顺应这一改革势在必行，否则他们必无法斩获人皆渴求的赫赫声名。

我认为任何费心对所谓英雄行径的记述方式形成见解之人，必会发现其充满谬误；且必会痛惜两类著述者自荷马直至吉本几乎普遍误导了人类的道德自觉，将其视作社会的莫大不幸之一。依此视角，我们大体可以对诗人和史家作出同其笔下英雄一般的断言：他们对人道事业所造成的戕害，几乎同他们所攫取的名望成正比。

我作此断言绝非意指此类作家毫无贡献。即便是给人类造成的祸患甚于任何其他人的荷马史诗，亦同样是某一类益处的丰沛源泉。它们升华每一位读者的心智；历经数代岁月，在诗人、艺术家、哲人与英雄胸中激发了伟大的才智创举。然而必须审视的是，自其时代直至当今，它们亦同样是支配人类行径的虚妄荣誉观与荒谬施政体系的罪恶渊薮。

===Original===

If, instead of the *Iliad*, he had given us a work of equal splendor founded on an opposite principle, whose object should have been to celebrate the useful arts of agriculture and navigation; to build the immortal fame of his heroes and occupy his whole hierarchy of gods, on actions that contribute to the real advancement of society, instead of striking away every foundation on which society ought to be established or can be greatly advanced; mankind, enriched with such a work at that early period, would have given a useful turn to their ambition through all succeeding ages.

It is not easy to conceive how different the state of nations would have been at this day from what we now find it, had such a bent been given to the pursuits of genius and such glory cast upon actions truly worthy of imitation. I have treated this subject more at large in the third chapter of *Advice to the Privileged Orders*.

But it will be asked how this kind of censure can attach to the writers of history, whose business is to invent nothing, to confine themselves to the simple narration of facts and relate the actions of men, not as they should be, but as they are. This is indeed a part of the duty of the historian; but it is not his whole duty. His narrative should be clear and simple; but he should likewise develop the political and moral tendency of the transactions he details.

In reviewing actions or doctrines which favor despotism, injustice, false morals or political errors, he should not suffer them to pass without an open and well supported censure. He should show how the authors of such actions might have conducted themselves and succeeded in gaining the celebrity which they sought, by doing good instead of harm to the age and country where they acquired their fame.

===Chinese===

设若他当年未曾撰写《伊利亚特》，而是赋予我们一部建立在相反原则之上、享有同等辉煌的鸿篇巨制，其主旨旨在颂扬农耕与航海等致用技艺；将其英雄的流芳百世与整套神祇谱系，构筑在促进社会真正精进的功业之上，而非摧毁社会赖以建立或赖以宏大发展的每一基石；那么人类在早期阶段领受如此巨著的滋养，在其后所有世代中必将其抱负抱持引向致用正途。

设若才华的求索曾被赋予如此导向、真正值得效仿的功业被赋予如此光彩，今日万邦之境况较我们当下所见将呈现何等截然不同的面貌，实难凭空逆料。我在《告特权阶层书》第三章中对这一命题作了更详尽的阐述。

但有人必会探问：此类非难何以能加诸史家之身？史家的职责在于绝不虚构任何事物，局限于单纯陈述史实，如实记录人类的行径——非关其理应如何，唯论其实际怎样。这确是史家职责的一部分；但绝非其全部职责。他的叙事理当清晰明了；但他亦当揭示其所详述事件的政治与道德旨归。

在审视那些纵容专制、不义、虚妄道德或施政谬误的行径或学说时，他不应容许其未经公开且立论确凿的谴责便轻易蒙混过关。他应当指明此类行径的始作俑者本可如何自律行事，通过造福而非荼毒建功立业的时代与国度，同样博得其梦寐以求的显赫名望。

===Original===

The history of human actions, in a political view, has generally been the history of human errors. The writers who have given it to us do not appear to have been sensible of this. How then are young readers to be sensible of it? Their minds are still to be formed; and those who are destined for public life must in a great measure take their bias from the study of history. But history in general, to answer the purpose of sound instruction to the future guides of nations, must be rewritten. For example: among the hundred historians who have treated of what is called the Roman Republic I know not one who has told us this important fact, that Rome never had a Republic. The same may be said of Athens, and of several other turbulent associations of men in former ages. And it is for want of this attention or this knowledge in the writers of their histories, that the republican principle of government is so generally associated, even at this day, with the idea of insurrection, anarchy and the desire of conquest. Whereas it is in fact the *want* of the republican principle, not the *practice* of it, which has occasioned all the insurrections, anarchy and desire of conquest, that have disturbed the order of society both in ancient and modern times.

Again: in relating the destruction of Carthage, a measure which the zealous patriots, both before and after, considered so essential to the glory of the Roman state, and which has immortalized so many heroes as the authors and projectors of that destruction, I believe no historian has told us that the disease, decay and downfall of Rome itself were occasioned by that measure and must be dated from that epoch; and that the actions of Regulus and Scipio, the themes of universal applause, were really more injurious to their country than those of Marias and Sylla, the objects (and justly so) of universal detestation.

===Chinese===

从政治视角审视，人类行径的历史大体上便是一部人类谬误的历史。将历史呈现给我们的史家似乎并未意识到此点。那么年轻读者又何以能领悟此理？他们的心智尚待塑造；而那些注定投身公众生活之人，在极大程度上必从历史研习中汲取其倾向偏好。但总体而言，历史若要达成对未来国家领路人施以健全训导的目的，便必须予以重写。例如：在论及所谓罗马共和国的百位史家中，我不知有何人曾告知我们这一重大史实——罗马从未真正有过共和国。对于雅典以及往昔数个动荡混乱的人群聚落，亦可作如是观。正是因其历史记述者缺乏此种审视或缺乏此种认知，共和政体的原则甚至在今日仍普遍同叛乱、无政府状态与对外征服的欲望联系在一起。然而事实上，正是因缺乏共和原则而非践行该原则，才酿成了在古今时代扰乱社会秩序的所有叛乱、混乱与征服野心。

再如：在记述迦太基的覆灭时（此项举措在当时乃至其后皆被热忱爱国者视作关乎罗马国运荣耀之关键，并使诸多作为该毁灭策划者与倡导者的英雄名垂青史），我相信尚无史家曾告诉我们：罗马自身的病态、衰亡与倾覆，皆是由那项举措所酿成，且其年代必自那一纪元算起；而普世喝彩的雷古鲁斯与西庇阿的功业，对自身祖国造成的戕害，实际上远甚于遭受举世公认唾弃的马略与苏拉的恶行。

===Original===

If these principles had been understood by Polybius and his successors in the brilliant heritage of history and had been properly impressed on the minds of their readers, we should not have heard old Cato’s vociferation *delenda est Carthago* applied to the American states by an orator of the British parliament, as we did during the war; because every member of that parliament must have understood that the prosperity of these states would be highly advantageous to Britain, from the extensive commercial intercourse that the relative situation of the two countries required. Neither should we see at this day the French and English nations seeking to impoverish and extirpate each other; each of them entertaining the erroneous and absurd opinion that its own prosperity is to be increased by the adversity of its neighbor. We should have learned long ago from the plain dictates of reason, instead of having it beat into us some ages hence by costly experience, that the true dignity of a state is in the happiness of its members; and that their happiness is best promoted by the pursuit of industry at home and the free exchange of their productions abroad.

We should have perceived the real and constant interest that every nation has in the prosperity of its neighbors, instead of their destruction. France would have perceived that the wealth of the English would be beneficial to her, by enabling them to receive and pay for more of her produce. England would have seen the same thing with regard to the French; and such would have been the sentiments of other nations reciprocally and universally.

I know I must be called an extravagant theorist if I insinuate that all these good things would have resulted from having history well written and poetry well conceived. No man will doubt however that such would have been the tendency; nor can we deny that the contrary has resulted, at least in some degree, from the manner in which such writings have been composed. And why should we write at all, if not to benefit mankind? The public mind, as well as the individual mind, receives its propensities; it is equally the creature of habit. Nations are educated, like a single child. They only require a longer time and a greater number of teachers. ↩︎

-

===Chinese===

设若波利比乌斯及其在辉煌历史遗产中的继承者曾通晓这些原则，并在其读者心智中留下深刻印记，我们便不会在战事期间听闻一位英国议会演说家将老加图「迦太基必须毁灭」的喧嚣叫嚣套用在美洲各邦身上；因该议会的每一位议员必当明晓，鉴于两国相对区位所要求的广泛商贸往来，这些邦国的繁荣对不列颠而言将具有无可估量的巨大利益。今日我们亦不致目睹英法两国竭力令彼此贫困绝灭；各自怀抱着自身繁荣必依仗邻邦受难而增进的荒谬错误之见。我们早在很久以前便当从理性的明晰昭示中汲取教益（而非在数个时代之后通过惨痛代价强行灌输给我们）：国家的真正尊严在于其臣民的福祉；而其福祉的最佳促进之道，在于对内精勤实业、对外自由交换产品。

我们本当看清每个国家在邻邦繁荣而非毁灭中所享有的真实而持久的利益。法兰西本当看清英格兰人的财富将有益于自身，使后者能够接收并支付更多法国出产。英格兰在看待法兰西时本亦能领悟同一道理；而其他国家之间的彼此见解亦当互惠且普世如此。

我知晓若我暗示所有这些善果本皆可通过精心撰写历史与精妙构思诗歌而达成，必会被人斥为异想天开的空想家。然而无人能怀疑这本会是必然的导向；我们亦不能否认反向结果的产生（至少在某种程度上）正是源于此类著作的草率编撰方式。而我们若非为了造福人类，又何必著书立说？公众心智同个体心智一样，接受其特定倾向；它同样是习惯的产物。教化万邦宛如教育孤单孩童。它们唯独需要更为悠长的时光，以及更多数量的明师。 ↩︎

-

===Original===

Absurdities in speculative opinion are commonly considered as innocent things; and we are told every day that they are not worth refuting. So far as opinions are sure to rest merely in speculation and cannot in any degree become practical, this is doubtless the proper way of treating them. But there are few opinions of this dormant and indifferent kind, especially among those that become general and classical among the nations.

The activity of such, though imperceptible, is extensive. They get wrought into our intellectual existence and govern our modes of acting as well as thinking. The interest of society therefore requires that they should be scrutinized, and that such as are erroneous should be exposed, in order to be rejected; when their place may be supplied by truth and reason, which nourish the mind and accelerate the progress of improvement.

Among the absurd notions which early turned the heads of the teachers of mankind and which are so ridiculous as generally to escape our censure, is that of a Golden Age; or the idea that men were more perfect, more moral and more happy in some early stage of their intercourse, before they cultivated the earth and formed great societies.

The author of *Don Quixote* has played his artillery upon this doctrine to very good effect; he has summoned against it all the force of our contempt by making it the text of one of the gravest discourses of his hero. But my sensibility is such on moral and political errors, as rarely to be satisfied with the weapon of ridicule; though I know it to be one of the most mortal of intellectual weapons.

The notion that the social state of men cannot ameliorate, that they have formerly been better than they now are, and that they are continually growing worse, is pregnant with infinite mischief. I know no doctrine in the whole labyrinth of imposture that has a more immoral tendency. It discourages the efforts of all political virtue; it is a constant and practical apology for oppression, tyranny, despotism, in every shape, in every corner of society, as well as from the throne, the pulpit, the tribunal and the camp. It inculcates the belief that ignorance is better than knowledge; that war and violence are more natural than industry and peace; that deserts and tombs are more glorious than joyful cities and cultivated fields.

===Chinese===

思辨见解中的荒谬通常被视作无伤大雅之物；人们每日被告诫此类见解不值一驳。只要见解确定仅止于思辨、且绝不可能在任何程度上付诸实践，这无疑是处置它们的恰当方式。但此类蛰伏冷漠的见解实属罕见，尤以在万国之中成为普世经典的见解为然。

此类见解的活性虽不着痕迹，却影响深远。它们渗透进我们的智识存在之中，支配着我们的行事与思考模式。因而社会的福祉要求对它们严加审视，剥去谬误的面具以便予以摒弃；届时其位置便可由滋养心智并加速改良进程的真理与理性所取代。

在很早便令世间导师神魂颠倒、且荒谬至通常免遭我们责难的离奇见解中，便有所谓「黄金时代」的幻象；亦即设想人类在其交往的早期阶段——在耕耘土地和组成大同社会之前——更为完善、更具道德且更为幸福。

《堂吉诃德》作者曾向这一学说倾泻了极富实效的猛烈炮火；他通过将该学说作为其主人公最庄重的宏论之一，调动起我们蔑视的全部伟力。但面对道德与政治上的谬误，我个人的敏锐感受却罕能满足于嘲讽的利刃；尽管我深知它是最具杀伤力的智识武器之一。

人类社会状态不可改良、往昔强于当今且日趋每况愈下的论调，孕育着无穷无尽的祸患。纵观整个欺诈迷宫，我不知有任何学说具有更为败坏道德的倾向。它挫伤了所有政治美德的探索；它是对一切形式、社会各个角落——以及来自宝座、讲坛、法庭与军营——的压迫、暴政与专制的恒常且切实的辩护词。它灌输愚昧胜于知识的信念；断言战争与暴力比实业与和平更加契合天性；宣扬荒漠与坟冢比欢欣的城池与耕耘的田原更为光荣辉煌。

===Original===

One of the most operative means of bringing forward our improvements and of making mankind wiser and better than they are, is to convince them that they are capable of becoming so. Without this conviction they may indeed improve slowly, unsteadily and almost imperceptibly, as they have done within the period in which our histories are able to trace them. But this conviction, impressed on the minds of the chiefs and teachers of nations, and inculcated in their schools, would greatly expedite our advancement in public happiness and virtue. Perhaps it would in a great measure insure the world against any future shocks and retrograde steps, such as heretofore it has often experienced. ↩︎

===Chinese===

推动我们精进改良、使人类比现状更为明智优良的最有效手段之一，乃是令他们确信自己有能力臻于此境。若无此信念，他们诚然可能缓慢、蹒跚且几乎不可察觉地前行，正如在我们的史书所能考索的岁月里所经历的那样。然而一旦将这一信念铭刻在万国领袖与明师的心智之中，并灌输于学校讲堂之上，必将极大促进我们在公共福祉与美德上的迈进。或许它将在极大程度上护佑世界免遭未来任何剧烈震荡与历史倒退，正如往昔屡屡所遭遇的那样。 ↩︎

===Original===

Tu spiegherai, Colombo, a un novo polo

Lontane sì le fortunate antenne,

Ch’a pena seguirà con gli occhi il volo

La Fama, ch’ hà mille occhi e mille penne.

Canti ella Alcide, e Bacco; e di te solo

Basti a i posteri tuoi, ch’ alquanto accenne:

Chè quel poco darà lunga memoria

Di poema degnissima, e d’istoria.

> *
> 						Gierus.
> 						Lib.
> 					*
> 					Can.
> 					XV

===Chinese===

哥伦布啊，你将驶向崭新的极地，

舒展你那万里远航的福佑之帆，

纵使那生有千目千羽的声誉女神，

其目光亦难紧随你奋飞的身影。

任她颂扬阿尔喀得斯与巴克科斯；唯独对你，

只要向你的后世略作提及便已足矣：

因那区区片语便将流芳百世，

无愧于最崇高的史诗与信史。

> 塔索《被解救的耶路撒冷》第十五歌

## Introduction / 引言
===Original===

Every circumstance relating to the discovery and settlement of America is an interesting object of inquiry, especially to the great and growing nations of this hemisphere, who owe their existence to those arduous labors. Yet it is presumed that many persons, who might be entertained with a poem on this subject, are but slightly acquainted with the life and character of the hero whose extraordinary genius led him to discover the continent, and whose singular sufferings, arising from that service, ought to excite the indignation of the world.

Christopher Columbus was born in Genoa about the year 1447, when the navigation of Europe was scarcely extended beyond the limits of the Mediterranean and the other narrow seas that border the great ocean. The mariner’s compass had been invented and in common use for more than a century; yet with the help of this sure guide, and prompted by a laudable spirit of discovery, the mariners of those days rarely ventured from the sight of land. They acquired wonderful applause by sailing along the coast of Africa, and discovering some of the neighboring islands; and after pushing their researches with great industry for half a century, the Portuguese, who were the most fortunate and enterprising, extended their voyages southward no farther than the equator.

The rich commodities of the East had, for several ages, been brought into Europe by the Red Sea and the Mediterranean; and it had now become the object of the Portuguese to find a passage to India by sailing round the southern extremity of Africa, and then taking an eastern course. This great object engaged the general attention, and drew into the Portuguese service adventurers from the other maritime nations of Europe. Every year added to their experience in navigation, and seemed to promise some distant reward to their industry. The prospect, however, of arriving at India by that route was still by no means encouraging. Fifty years’ perseverance in the same track having brought them only to the equator, it was probable that as many more would elapse before they could accomplish their purpose.

===Chinese===

关涉亚美利加（America）发现与开拓拓殖的每一端细节，皆是令人神往的探究命题，对于本半球那些有赖于彼等艰苦劳作方得以立国的伟大而日新月异之国度而言尤为如此。然而可以料想的是，诸多或能因一部以此为题材的诗作而陶怡身心之人，对那位凭借非凡天才发现这一新大陆的英雄之生平与品性却知之甚少，而他因建立该功勋所遭受的离奇凄苦，更理当激发全世界的愤慨。

克里斯托弗·哥伦布（Christopher Columbus）约于1447年生于热那亚，彼时欧洲（Europe）的航海事业几乎尚未越出地中海及濒临大洋的其他狭窄海域之范畴。航海罗盘已发明并通行了一个多世纪；然而纵有这一定盘指南针之指引，且在值得称道的探险精神激励之下，当年的航海者仍鲜敢驶离陆地视线之外。他们因沿非洲海岸巡航并发现若干邻近岛屿而博得满堂喝彩；在半个世纪中极尽勤勉地推展探索之后，最受幸运眷顾且最敢于进取的葡萄牙人，其南向航海所涉亦未越出赤道之外。

东方丰饶的奇珍异宝数代以来皆经由红海与地中海运抵欧洲；此时找寻一条环绕非洲最南端并继而向东航行通向印度的航道，便成为了葡萄牙人的宏愿目标。这一重大目标吸引了举世的瞩目，并将欧洲其他航海诸国的探险家招揽入葡萄牙的麾下效力。岁岁年年的远航增长了他们的航海阅历，并似乎有望为其勤勉带来某种遥远的回报。然而循该路线抵达印度的前景仍旧难言乐观。在同一条航线上坚持了五十年仅令他们推进至赤道，要达成其宿愿，极可能仍需耗费同样漫长的岁月。

===Original===

But Columbus, by an uncommon exertion of genius, formed a design no less astonishing to the age in which he lived than beneficial to posterity. This design was to sail to India by taking a western direction. By the accounts of travellers who had visited that part of Asia, it seemed almost without limits on the east; and by attending to the spherical figure of the earth, Columbus drew the natural conclusion, that the Atlantic ocean must be bounded on the west either by India itself, or by some continent not far distant from it.

This illustrious navigator, who was then about twenty-seven years of age, appears to have possessed every talent requisite to form and execute the greatest enterprises. He was early educated in such of the useful sciences as were taught in that day. He had made great proficiency in geography, astronomy and drawing, as they were necessary to his favorite pursuit of navigation. He had been a number of years in the service of the Portuguese, and had acquired all the experience that their voyages and discoveries could afford. His courage had been put to the severest test; and the exercise of every amiable as well as heroic virtue, the kindred qualities of a great mind, had secured him an extensive reputation. He had married a Portuguese lady, by whom he had two sons, Diego and Ferdinand; the younger of these is the historian of his father’s life.

Such was the situation of Columbus, when he formed and digested a plan, which, in its operation and consequences, has unfolded to the view of mankind one half of the globe, diffused wealth and industry over the other, and is extending commerce and civilization through the whole. To corroborate the theory he had formed of the existence of a western continent, his discerning mind, which knew the application of every circumstance that fell in his way, had observed several facts which by others would have passed unnoticed. In his voyages to the African islands he had found, floating ashore after a long western storm, pieces of wood carved in a curious manner, canes of a size unknown in that quarter of the world, and human bodies with very singular features.

===Chinese===

但哥伦布凭借非凡卓越的天才，构想出了一项对于其所处世代惊世骇俗、而对于后世却受惠无穷的壮举蓝图。这一设想便是向西航行直达印度。依造访过亚洲那一区域的旅人记述，其东方疆域似乎漫无边际；而审度大地的球形形态，哥伦布得出了一个顺理成章的推断：大西洋（Atlantic）以西的尽头，要么是印度本土，要么是相距不远的某座大陆。

这位当时年约二十七岁的杰出航海家，似乎兼备了构思并付诸实施最宏大进取事业所必需的一切才干。他早年便修习了那个时代所传授的致用学科。他在地理学、天文学与绘图术上造诣深厚，因这些皆是他钟爱的航海事业所不可或缺的。他在葡萄牙海军服役多年，掌握了其远航与发现所能提供的一切经验。他的勇气曾受过最严酷的淬炼；对每一种温良与英雄美德的践行（此乃伟大心智相通的品格），为他赢得了广泛的声誉。他迎娶了一位葡萄牙女子为妻，育有二子，分别名为迭戈与斐迪南；幼子即其父生平之史家。

哥伦布正是在这般境遇之下，构思并完善了一项大计，该计划在其运作与影响中，向人类视界揭开了地球的半壁江山，将财富与实业播撒至另一半，并正将贸易与文明拓展至整个寰宇。为证实自己构想的西方大陆存在论，他那洞察秋毫的心智（善于将身畔降临的一切境遇化为致用考证）察觉到了诸多常人视而不见的实证。在远航非洲群岛途中，他在连日西向风暴后漂流至滩头的杂物中，发现了雕琢奇特的木块、该半球闻所未闻的粗大藤竹，以及容貌异乎寻常的浮尸骸骨。

===Original===

The opinion being well established in his mind that a considerable portion of the earth still remained to be discovered, his temper was too vigorous and persevering to suffer an idea of this importance to rest merely in speculation, as it had done with Plato and Seneca, who seem to have entertained conjectures of a similar nature. He determined, therefore, to bring his theory to the test of experiment. But an object of that magnitude required the patronage of a prince; and a design so extraordinary met with all the obstructions that an age of superstition could invent, and personal jealousy enhance.

It is happy for mankind that, in this instance, a genius capable of devising the greatest undertakings associated in itself a degree of patience and enterprise, modesty and confidence, which rendered him superior to these misfortunes, and enabled him to meet with fortitude all the future calamities of his life. Excited by an ardent enthusiasm to become a discoverer of new countries, and fully sensible of the advantages that would result to mankind from such discoveries, he had the cruel mortification to wear away eighteen years of his life, after his system was well established in his own mind, before he could obtain the means of executing his projected voyage. The greatest part of this period was spent in successive solicitations in Genoa, Portugal and Spain.

===Chinese===

在其心智中既然已牢固确立了大地仍有相当部分尚待发现之信念，他的天性便过于坚毅沉稳，绝难容忍此等重大构想仅仅止步于空想思辨（正如柏拉图与塞涅卡往昔所为，二人似乎亦曾抱有类似的推想）。因此他决意将自己的学说付诸行动的检验，并立誓要么找寻到一片大陆，要么死在探索的征程中。

他虽具备一切足以使大业告成的资质，却缺乏启动大业所必需的资财。他遂向热那亚共和国求助，但遭到回绝。随后他将计划呈报葡萄牙国王若昂二世，国王虽怀有进取的远见，却受教于一众心胸狭隘之人，那些人以虚与委蛇之辞将哥伦布拖延羁绊，同时暗中派遣一艘轻快帆船意图窃取其构想的荣耀，抢先一步完成发现。然而这支远征队的统帅既无胆魄亦无见识；在航行数日遭遇风暴之后，他便折返里斯本，将该计划斥为荒诞狂想予以嘲笑。

===Original===

As a duty to his native country he made his first proposal to the senate of Genoa, where it was soon rejected. Conscious of the truth of his theory, and of his own abilities to execute his plan, he retired without dejection from a body of men who were incapable of forming any just ideas upon the subject, and applied with fresh confidence to John Second, king of Portugal, who had distinguished himself as the great patron of navigation, and in whose service Columbus had acquired a reputation which entitled him and his project to general confidence. But here he experienced a treatment much more insulting than a direct refusal. After referring the examination of his scheme to the council who had the direction of naval affairs, and drawing from him his general ideas of the length of the voyage and the course he meant to take, that splendid monarch had the meanness to conspire with this council to rob Columbus of the glory and advantage he expected to derive from his undertaking. While Columbus was amused with the negotiation, in hopes of having his scheme adopted, a vessel was secretly dispatched by order of the king to make the intended discovery. Want of skill or courage in the pilot rendered the plot unsuccessful; and Columbus, on discovering the treachery, retired with an ingenuous indignation from a court which could be capable of such duplicity.

Having now performed what was due to the country that gave him birth, and to the one that had adopted him as a subject, he was at liberty to court the patronage of any other which should have the wisdom to accept his proposals. He had communicated his ideas to his brother Bartholomew, whom he sent to England to negotiate with Henry Seventh; at the same time he went himself into Spain to apply in person to Ferdinand and Isabella, who governed the united kingdoms of Aragon and Castile.

===Chinese===

哥伦布对这种背信弃义的勾当深恶痛绝，当即毅然绝尘离开葡萄牙，动身前往西班牙（Spain）。与此同时，他派遣胞弟巴托洛梅前往英格兰，向亨利七世呈报其宏伟蓝图。巴托洛梅在航程中遭遇海盗洗劫，沦落至伦敦贫病交加；他历经数年方得以向国王呈报计划，而国王倒是颇为明智地接纳了他的呈请。

倘若这桩差使未曾横遭变故，亨利七世必将赞助此次远征，而发现新大陆的无上荣耀与丰厚利益亦将归于英格兰。

===Original===

The circumstances of his brother’s application in England, which appears to have been unsuccessful, are not to my purpose to relate; and the limits prescribed to this biographical sketch will prevent the detail of particulars respecting his own negotiation in Spain. This occupied him eight years; in which the various agitations of suspense, expectation and disappointment must have borne hard upon his patience. At length his scheme was adopted by Isabella; who undertook, as queen of Castile, to defray the expenses of the expedition, and declared herself ever after the friend and patron of the hero who projected it.

Columbus, who during his ill success in the negotiation never abated anything of the honors and emoluments which he expected to acquire in the expedition, obtained from Ferdinand and Isabella a stipulation of every article contained in his first proposals. He was constituted high admiral and viceroy of all the seas, islands and continents which he should discover; with power to receive one tenth of the profits arising from their productions and commerce, which offices and emoluments were to be made hereditary in his family.

===Chinese===

本传略篇幅所限，难以详述他在西班牙长达八年的斡旋细节；在此期间，悬心未决、热切期盼与失望幻灭的轮番折磨，必极大考验了他的忍耐力。最终他的计划获得了伊莎贝拉（Isabella）的采纳；伊莎贝拉以卡斯蒂利亚（Castile）女王的身份，承诺承担远征的全部开销，并自此宣布成为构想这一壮举的英雄的永世挚友与赞助人。

哥伦布在谈判受阻期间，对自己期许在远征中所斩获的荣耀与禄位从未作丝毫退让，终于从斐迪南（Ferdinand）与伊莎贝拉手中争取到了对其初始呈文中每一条款的明文允诺。他被任命为所发现之一切海洋、岛屿与大陆的统帅大提督与总督；有权抽取其出产与商贸所得利润的十分之一，且此类官职与俸禄皆世袭其家族。

===Original===

These articles being adjusted, the preparations for the voyage were brought forward with rapidity; but they were by no means adequate to the importance of the expedition. Three small vessels, scarcely sufficient in size to be employed in the coasting business, were appointed to traverse the vast Atlantic, and to encounter the storms and currents always to be expected in tropical climates, uncertain seasons and unknown seas. These vessels, as we must suppose them in the infancy of navigation, were ill constructed, in a poor condition, and manned by seamen unaccustomed to distant voyages. But the tedious length of time which Columbus had passed in solicitation and suspense, and the prospect of being able soon to obtain the object of his wishes, induced him to overlook what he could not easily remedy, and led him to disregard those circumstances which would have intimidated any other mind. He accordingly equipped his small squadron with as much expedition as possible, manned with ninety men and victualled for one year. With these, on the third of August 1492, amidst a vast crowd of spectators, he set sail on an enterprise which, if we consider the ill condition of his ships, the inexperience of his sailors, the length and precarious nature of his voyage and the consequences that flowed from it, was the most daring and important that ever was undertaken. He touched at some of the Portuguese settlements in the Canary isles; where, although he had been but a few days at sea, he found his vessels needed refitting. He soon made the necessary repairs, and took his departure from the westermost islands that had hitherto been discovered. Here he left the former track of navigation, and steered his course due west.

===Chinese===

条款底定之后，航行的筹备工作迅即展开；然而这些筹备同远征的重大意义相比却显得极其寒酸。三艘小船（其尺寸单用于沿海航运尚嫌勉强）被指定用于横渡辽阔的大西洋，直面热带气候、多变时令与未知海域中注定会遭遇的惊涛骇浪与洋流暗礁。这些船只作为航海初期的产物，建造拙劣，船况破败，且配属着未曾经历过远洋航行的水手。然而哥伦布在奔走恳求与悬心煎熬中所虚度的漫长岁月，以及即将达成平生夙愿的前景，促使他包容了无法轻易更正的缺陷，并不顾一切令常人望而却步的艰险。他遂以尽可能最迅捷的速度装备了他的微型小舰队，配属九十名船员，备足一年粮饷。1492年8月3日，在万头攒动的围观人群瞩目下，他率领他们扬帆起航，踏上了一场若审度其船况之破败、水手之生疏、航程之漫长艰险及其所引发之深远后果、堪称人类历史上最英勇壮烈且最重要的伟大征程。他在加那利群岛的葡萄牙定居点停靠；在彼处出海仅数日，他便发现船只必须大修。他迅即完成必要修缮，自迄今所发现的最西端岛屿拔锚起航。至此他抛却了往昔航海的所有旧径，驾驭船舵直指南方正西。

===Original===

Not many days after he laid this course he perceived the symptoms of a new scene of difficulty. The sailors now began to contemplate the dangers and uncertain issue of a voyage, the nature and length of which were left entirely open to conjecture. Besides the fickleness and timidity natural to men unaccustomed to the discipline of a seafaring life, several circumstances contributed to inspire an obstinate and mutinous disposition, which required the most consummate art as well as fortitude in the admiral to control. Having been three weeks at sea, and experienced the uniform course of the trade winds, they contended that, should they continue the same course for a longer time, the same winds would never permit them to return to Spain. The magnetic needle began to vary its direction. This being the first time that this phenomenon was ever noticed, it was viewed by the sailors with astonishment; they thought it an indication that nature itself had changed its laws, and that Providence was about to punish their audacity in venturing so far beyond the bounds of man. They declared that the commands of the government had been fully obeyed in their proceeding so many days in the same course, and so far surpassing all former navigators in quest of discoveries.

Every talent requisite for governing, soothing and tempering the passions of men is conspicuous in the conduct of Columbus on this occasion. The dignity and affability of his manners, his surprising knowledge and experience in naval affairs, his unwearied and minute attention to the duties of his command, gave him a great ascendency over the minds of his men, and inspired that degree of confidence which would have maintained his authority in almost any circumstances. But here, from the nature of the undertaking, every man had leisure to feed his imagination with the gloominess and uncertainty of the prospect. They found from day to day the same steady gales wafting them with rapidity from their native country, and indeed from all countries of which they had any knowledge.

===Chinese===

沿此航线航行未逾数日，他便察觉到了崭新困境的征兆。水手们此时开始盘算起这场性质与航程全凭猜测的航行中所蕴含的凶险与叵测结局。除未经航海生活纪律约束之人天生的反复无常与怯懦之外，数种情势更是激起了一种冥顽不化的哗变苗头，需要大提督施展极尽精湛的权谋与刚毅心志方能加以弹压。出海已有三周，历经了信风恒常不变的吹拂，他们坚称设若继续沿该航线航行更久，这同一阵风将永远不允许他们重返西班牙。磁罗盘开始偏离方向。此乃该现象有史以来首次为人所察觉，水手们见状惊骇万状；他们视其为大自然本身更迭法则的凶兆，意味着上天即将惩罚他们涉足如此遥远逾越人间界限的狂妄。他们宣称连续多日沿同一航向航行早已完全遵从了朝廷的号令，且在探寻新大陆的征程上早已远远超越了往昔的所有航海先辈。

统摄、安抚并陶冶众人激情的每一种必备才干，在哥伦布此时的举止中皆展现得淋漓尽致。他高贵而平易近人的风度、在海事上令人惊叹的渊博学识与丰富阅历、对其指挥职责不知疲倦且明察秋毫的专注于行，赋予他对麾下部众心智的巨大统摄力，并激发出足以在几乎任何险境下皆能维系其威望的崇高信任。然而在此处，源于探险本身的天性，每个人皆有闲暇以暗淡叵测的前景滋养内心的忧惧阴霾。他们发现日复一日正是同一阵恒常劲风，将他们飞速吹离故土家园，吹离他们所知晓的世间所有国度。

===Original===

He addressed himself to their passions with all the variety of management that the situation would admit, sometimes by soothing them with the prognostics of approaching land, sometimes by flattering their ambition and feasting their avarice with the glory and wealth they would acquire from discovering the rich countries beyond the Atlantic, and sometimes by threatening them with the displeasure of their king, should their disobedience defeat so great an object. But every argument soon lost its effect; and their uneasiness still increased. From secret whisperings it arose to open mutiny and dangerous conspiracy. At length they determined to rid themselves of the remonstrances of Columbus by throwing him into the sea. The infection spread from ship to ship, and involved officers as well as sailors. They finally lost all sense of subordination and addressed their commander in an insolent manner, demanding to be conducted immediately back to Spain; or, they assured him, they would seek their own safety by taking away his life.

Columbus, whose sagacity had discerned every symptom of the disorder, was prepared for this last stage of it; and was sufficiently apprized of the danger that awaited him. He found it vain to contend with passions he could no longer control. He therefore proposed that they should obey his orders for three days longer; and should they not discover land in that time, he would then direct his course for Spain. They complied with his proposal; and, happily for mankind, in three days they discovered land. This was a small island, to which he gave the name of San Salvador. His first interview with the natives was a scene of compassion on the one part and astonishment on the other, but highly interesting to both. The natives were entirely naked, simple and timorous; and they viewed the Spaniards as a superior order of beings descended from the sun; which, in that island and in most parts of America, was worshipped as a deity. By this it was easy for Columbus to perceive the line of conduct proper to be observed toward that simple and inoffensive people. Had his companions and successors of the Spanish nation possessed the wisdom and humanity of this great discoverer, the benevolent mind would have had to experience no sensations of regret in contemplating the extensive advantages arising to mankind from the discovery of America.

===Chinese===

他顺应局势所容许的各般策略诉诸他们的心绪：时而以临近陆地的吉兆安抚他们，时而迎合其抱负、以发现大西洋彼岸富庶国度将攫取的荣耀与财富挑逗其贪婪，时而以抗命将断送宏大伟业而引致君王震怒威吓他们。然而一切说辞很快便化为乌有；他们的焦躁不安仍持续攀升。从暗中窃窃私语演变至公开哗变与危险谋逆。最终他们决意将哥伦布掷入大海，以摆脱其劝诫。这股哗变恶风在各船只间蔓延，将军官与水手尽皆卷入其中。他们最终丧失了臣服的全部意识，以极其傲慢无礼的态度质问其指挥官，勒令即刻返航西班牙；否则他们扬言将取其性命以求自保。

哥伦布凭借非凡远见早已洞察这一动荡的每一细微征候，对这一最终危局早有准备，并深知等候着自己的凶险。他发觉同再也无法遏制的狂怒对抗实属徒劳。因此他提议他们遵其号令再航行三日；设若在此期间未能发现陆地，他便调转船头返航西班牙。他们顺从了他的提议；而对于全人类之大幸而言，在第三日他们便发现了陆地。那是一座小岛，他为其定名为圣萨尔瓦多。他与当地土著的首次相逢，是一幕一方心怀悲悯、另一方惊叹交加的场景，却对双方而言皆扣人心弦。土著赤身裸体、淳朴温良且胆怯怕事；他们将西班牙人视作自太阳降临的高等神灵——太阳在该岛及亚美利加绝大多数地域皆被奉为神祇。由此哥伦布轻而易举便领会了对待这一淳朴无害族群所理当遵循的行事准则。假若他的西班牙同僚与后继者皆具备这位伟大发现者的明智与仁爱，那么在审视发现亚美利加为人类带来的广阔福祉时，仁慈的心扉便无需承受任何痛惜与懊悔之感。

===Original===

In this voyage Columbus discovered the islands of Cuba and Hispaniola, on the latter of which he erected a small fort; and having left a garrison of thirty-eight men he set sail for Spain. Returning across the Atlantic, he was overtaken by a violent storm, which lasted several days, and increased to such a degree as baffled his naval skill and threatened immediate destruction. In this situation, when all were in a state of despair, and it was expected that every sea would swallow up the crazy vessel, he manifested a serenity and presence of mind seldom equalled in cases of like extremity. He wrote a short account of his voyage and of the discoveries he had made; this he hastily wrapped in an oiled cloth, then enclosed it in a cake of wax and put it into an empty cask, which he threw overboard, in hopes that some fortunate accident might preserve a deposit of so much importance to the world.

The storm however abated, and he at length arrived in Spain, after having been driven by stress of weather into the port of Lisbon; where he had opportunity, in an interview with the king of Portugal, to prove the truth of his system by arguments more convincing than those he had before advanced in the character of a bold projector but humble suitor. He was received everywhere in Spain with royal honors; his family was ennobled, and his former stipulation respecting his offices and emoluments was ratified in the most solemn manner by Ferdinand and Isabella; while all Europe resounded his praises, and reciprocated their joy and congratulations on the discovery of what they called a new world.

===Chinese===

在此次航行中，哥伦布发现了古巴岛与伊斯帕尼奥拉岛，并在后者构筑了一座小型要塞；在留下一支由三十八人组成的驻军后，他拔锚启程返航西班牙。在横渡大西洋返航途中，他遭遇了一场持续数日的狂暴飓风，风势之烈令其高超航海技艺亦无计可施，面临灭顶之灾。在此绝境之下，当众人陷入彻底绝望、料想每一道巨浪皆会将破旧船只吞噬之时，他显露出在类似险境中罕见的镇定与泰然气魄。他撰写了一份关于自身航程与所作发现的简略纪事；将其仓促包裹于油布之中，继而封入蜡丸并置入一只空木桶，随后投入大海，寄望某种幸运的机缘能为这方世界保全一份如此重大的珍贵遗产。

然而风暴最终平息，在因风暴所迫驶入里斯本港之后，他终于抵达西班牙；在彼处同葡萄牙国王的会晤中，他得以凭借比往昔作为大胆构想者兼卑微求告者更为令人信服的论据，证实了自身体系的真实不虚。他在西班牙各地皆受到王室般的崇隆礼遇；其家族获封贵族，他早先关于官衔与禄位的协议得到了斐迪南与伊莎贝拉最庄严的追认批准；而整个欧洲皆回荡着对他的颂扬，为发现世人所谓的「新大陆」而互致由衷的喜悦与庆贺。

===Original===

The immediate consequence was a second voyage, in which Columbus took charge of a squadron of seventeen ships of considerable burden. Volunteers of all ranks solicited to be employed in this expedition. He carried over fifteen hundred persons, with the necessaries for establishing a colony and extending his discoveries. In this voyage he explored most of the West India islands; but on his arrival at Hispaniola he found that the garrison he had left there had been all destroyed by the natives, and the fort demolished. He proceeded, however, in the planting of his colony; and, by his prudent and humane conduct towards the natives, he effectually established the Spanish authority in that island. But while he was thus laying the foundation of European dominion in America, some discontented persons, who had returned to Spain, uniting with his former opponents and powerful enemies at court, conspired to accomplish his ruin.

They represented his conduct in such a light as to create uneasiness in the jealous mind of Ferdinand, and make it necessary for Columbus again to return to Spain, to counteract their machinations and obtain such further supplies as were necessary to his great political and beneficent purposes. On his arriving at court, and stating with his usual dignity and confidence the whole history of his transactions abroad, everything wore a favorable appearance. He was received with the same honors as before, and solicited to take charge of another squadron, to carry out supplies, to pursue his discoveries, and in every respect to use his discretion in extending the Spanish empire in the new world.

In this third voyage he discovered the continent of America at the mouth of the river Orinoco. He rectified many disorders in his government of Hispaniola, which had happened in his absence; and everything was going on in a prosperous train, when an event was announced to him, which completed his own ruin and gave a fatal turn to the Spanish policy and conduct in America. This was the arrival of Francis de Bovadilla, with a commission to supersede Columbus in his government, to arraign him as a criminal, and pronounce judgment on all his former administration.

===Chinese===

直接的成果便是第二次航行，哥伦布在此次远征中统领了一支由十七艘大吨位船只组成的庞大舰队。各阶层自愿者皆争相乞求在远征队中谋得一席之地。他运载了一千五百人，携带着建立殖民地与拓展发现所必需的全部物资。在此次航行中他探查了西印度群岛的大部分岛屿；但在抵临伊斯帕尼奥拉岛时，他发现留守彼处的驻军已全被土著歼灭，要塞亦被夷平。然而他仍旧着手建立殖民定居点；并通过对待土著审慎仁道的举措，在该岛切实确立了西班牙的权柄。

随后他派遣大部分船只返回西班牙，并在其余船上装备了部分部众，着手探求新的发现。在此次巡航中他发现了牙买加岛，并在古巴海岸沿线进行了深度勘察。重返伊斯帕尼奥拉岛后，他发现由于其同僚残酷横暴的虐行，土著已纷纷揭竿而起反抗西班牙的统治。他们纠集了数万大军，企图将这些异邦人彻底消灭。

哥伦布仅率领手头由两百名步兵与二十名骑兵组成的微薄兵力，迎战这一庞大军队并将其彻底击溃。在确立起全面的和平与顺从之后，他将这片土地划分为各省，在各处构筑要塞，并在全岛推行了一套规整的贡赋制度。

===Original===

It seems that by this time the enemies of Columbus, despairing to complete his overthrow by groundless insinuations of malconduct, had taken the more effectual method of exciting the jealousy of their sovereigns. From the promising samples of gold and other valuable commodities brought from America, they took occasion to represent to the king and queen that the prodigious wealth and extent of the countries he had discovered would soon throw such power into the hands of the viceroy, that he would trample on the royal authority and bid defiance to the Spanish power. These arguments were well calculated for the cold and suspicious temper of Ferdinand, and they must have had some effect upon the mind of Isabella. The consequence was the appointment of Bovadilla, the inveterate enemy of Columbus, to take the government from his hands. This first tyrant of the Spanish nation in America began his administration by ordering Columbus to be put in chains on board of a ship, and sending him prisoner to Spain. By relaxing all discipline he introduced disorder and licentiousness through the colony. He subjected the unhappy natives to a most miserable servitude, and apportioned them out in large numbers among his adherents. Under this severe treatment perished in a short time many thousands of those innocent people.

Columbus was carried in his fetters to the Spanish court, where the king and queen either feigned or felt a sufficient regret at the conduct of Bovadilla towards their illustrious prisoner. He was not only released from confinement; he was treated with all imaginable respect. But, although the king endeavored to expiate the offence by censuring and recalling Bovadilla, yet we may judge of his sincerity from his appointing Nicholas de Ovando, another well known enemy of Columbus, to succeed in the government; and from his ever after refusing to reinstate Columbus, or to fulfil any of the conditions on which the discoveries had been undertaken.

===Chinese===

1498年他踏上了第三次航程。在此次远征中他发现了特立尼达岛，以及奥里诺科河畔的帕里亚海岸。自此他向西巡航直至玛格丽塔岛；在确立了大陆的存在之后，他折返伊斯帕尼奥拉岛。在彼处他发现殖民地因博瓦迪利亚等仇敌的挑唆，陷入了一场针对其兄弟管治的公开叛乱。

哥伦布成功平息了叛乱，并恢复了殖民地的秩序；但朝廷却被其仇敌的谗言所蒙蔽，派出了受命全权调查的法官。这名法官不仅没有支持大提督，反而逮捕了哥伦布及其兄弟，给他们戴上镣铐并押解回西班牙。船长出于对哥伦布的崇敬，提出在航行中为其卸下镣铐；但这位伟大的航海家骄傲地拒绝了这一提议，声称他唯有在君王直接号令下才会取下枷锁，且他将终生珍藏这些镣铐，作为其功勋所换取的赏赐。抵达西班牙后，民众对这一行径爆发了普遍的愤怒，伊莎贝拉女王下令立即释放哥伦布，并恢复其荣誉；然而斐迪南却并未真正惩处陷害他的恶徒，并剥夺了哥伦布对伊斯帕尼奥拉岛的治理权。

===Original===

After two years of solicitation for this or some other employment, he at length obtained a squadron of four small vessels to attempt new discoveries. He then set out, with the enthusiasm of a young adventurer, in quest of what was always his favorite object, a passage into the South Sea, by which he might sail to India. He touched at Hispaniola, where Ovando the governor refused him admittance on shore, even to take shelter during a hurricane, the prognostics of which his experience had taught him to discern. By putting into a creek he rode out the storm, and then bore away for the continent. He spent several months, the most boisterous of the year, in exploring the coast round the gulf of Mexico, in hopes of finding the intended navigation to India. At length he was shipwrecked and driven ashore on the island of Jamaica.

His cup of calamities seemed now to be full. He was cast upon an island of savages, without provisions, without a vessel, and thirty leagues from any Spanish settlement. But the greatest physical misfortunes are capable of being imbittered by the insults of our fellow creatures. A few of his companions generously offered, in two Indian canoes, to attempt a voyage to Hispaniola, in hopes of obtaining a vessel for the relief of the unhappy crew. After suffering every extremity of danger and fatigue, they arrived at the Spanish colony in ten days. Ovando, excited by personal malice against Columbus, detained these messengers for eight months, and then despatched a vessel to Jamaica to spy out the condition of Columbus and his crew, with positive instructions to the captain not to afford them any relief. This order was punctually executed. The captain approached the shore, delivered a letter of empty compliment from Ovando to the admiral, received his answer and returned. About four months afterwards a vessel came to their relief; and Columbus, worn out with fatigues and broken by misfortunes, returned for the last time to Spain. Here a new distress awaited him, which he considered as one of the greatest of his whole life: this was the death of Queen Isabella, his last and most powerful friend.

===Chinese===

在为争取该职务或其他任命奔走斡旋两年之后，他终于获准统领一支由四艘小船组成的舰队，以尝试展开新的发现。随后他怀着年轻探险家的热忱动身启程，探求其夙愿中的挚爱目标——通往南洋以扬帆驶向印度的通道。他在伊斯帕尼奥拉岛停靠，彼处总督奥万多却严拒其登岸，甚至在他依老练经验辨察出飓风凶兆欲求避风庇护时亦不予准许。他将船只驶入一处海湾熬过了风暴，随后拔锚驶向大陆。在一年中气候最恶劣的数月里，他探查了墨西哥湾周围的海岸，寄望找寻通向印度的预定通道。最终他遭遇海难，被迫漂流搁浅在牙买加岛上。

他的厄运苦杯此时似乎已彻底斟满。他漂落在一座野蛮部族聚居的孤岛上，缺乏粮饷，丧失船只，距任何西班牙定居点皆有三十里格之遥。然而最大的身心苦难，却往往因同胞的侮慢凌辱而变得更为苦涩。数名同僚慨然挺身而出，乘两艘印第安独木舟尝试航向伊斯帕尼奥拉岛，寄望能谋得一艘船只拯救苦难的船员。在历经了千难万险与极度疲惫之后，他们在十日内抵达了西班牙殖民地。奥万多出于对哥伦布的个人私怨，将这些使者扣押了八个月之久，其后派遣一艘船前往牙买加刺探哥伦布及其船员的境况，并向船长下达了绝不得给予其任何救援的严令。这一号令被不折不扣地执行了。船长靠近岸畔，向大提督递交了一封来自奥万多的客套空文，领受回信后便掉头离去。大约四个月后，一艘救援船只方才抵临；哥伦布身心俱疲，在厄运摧折下最后一次踏上归返西班牙之途。在彼处一场崭新的悲痛等待着他，被他视作毕生最沉重的打击之一：这便是他最后的强力靠山伊莎贝拉女王的溘然长逝。

===Original===

He did not suddenly abandon himself to despair. He called upon the gratitude and justice of the king; and in terms of dignity demanded the fulfilment of his former contract. Notwithstanding his age and infirmities, he even solicited to be further employed in extending the career of discovery, without a prospect of any other reward than the pleasure of doing good to mankind. But Ferdinand, cold, ungrateful and timid, dared not comply with any proposal of this kind, lest he should increase his own obligations to a man whose services he thought it dangerous to reward. He therefore delayed and avoided any decision on these subjects, in hopes that the declining health of Columbus would soon rid the court of the remonstrances of a suitor, whose unexampled merit was, in their opinion, a sufficient reason for destroying him. In this they were not disappointed. Columbus languished a short time, and gladly resigned a life which had been worn out in the most signal services, perhaps, that have been rendered by any one man to an ungrateful world.

Posterity is sometimes more just to the memory of great men than cotemporaries were to their persons. But even this consolation, if it is one, has been wanting to the discoverer of our hemisphere. The continent, instead of bearing his name, has been called after one of his followers, a man of no particular merit. And in the modern city of Mexico there is instituted and perpetuated, by order of government, an annual festival in honor of Hernando Cortez, the perfidious butcher of its ancient race; while no public honors have been decreed to Christopher Columbus, one of the wisest and best among the benefactors of mankind.

After his last return from America he seems to have passed the short remainder of his life at Valladolid, the capital of Old Castile, and then the seat of the Spanish government. He died in that city on the twentieth of August 1506, and was buried in one of its churches. Over his body is a plain stone inscribed simply with his name, as it is written in Spanish, **Christoval Colon**.

His son, who wrote his life, has left us a particular description of his person, manners and private character; all of which were agreeable and interesting. His portrait is in possession of the author of this poem. It is painted in oil, half length and the size of life, copied from an original picture in the gallery of Florence.

===Chinese===

他并未猝然放弃希望陷入绝望。他诉诸国王的感恩与公义；以尊严之辞要求履行早先的条约。尽管年迈体弱，他甚至恳求继续受命拓展探险发现之事业，除为人类谋福祉之欣慰外不求任何其他赏赐。然而冷酷、忘恩负义且怯懦的斐迪南，却绝不敢应允任何此类呈请，唯恐增添对一个他认为给予赏赐乃是危险之举的人的亏欠。因此他在这些命题上百般推诿拖延，寄望哥伦布日渐衰败的健康能很快令朝廷摆脱这位申诉者的陈情，而后者空前绝后的功勋在他们看来恰是除掉他的充分借口。在这一点上他们未曾失望。哥伦布在辗转病榻不久后便欣然告别了尘世，而这一生，或许正是倾注在一个凡人所能向忘恩负义的世界奉献的最显赫功勋之中耗尽的。

后世对于伟人记忆的公允，有时远甚于同时代人对其肉身之评判。然而即便是这份慰藉（若可算作慰藉的话），对我们半球的发现者而言亦荡然无存。这片大陆未曾铭刻他的名号，反倒依其一名并无特殊建树的追随者而命名。在现代墨西哥城，朝廷下令设立并长年举办一年一度的庆典，以纪念灭绝其古老族裔的奸诈刽子手埃尔南多·科尔特斯；而对于全人类最英明卓绝的施恩者之一克里斯托弗·哥伦布，却未曾颁赐任何公共荣耀。

自亚美利加最后一次归来后，他似乎在旧卡斯蒂利亚首府、时为西班牙政府驻地的巴利亚多利德度过了余生的短暂岁月。他于1506年8月20日逝世于该城，葬于城内一座教堂之中。其遗体之上仅立有一块简朴的石碑，朴素地刻写着他的西班牙文名号克里斯托瓦尔·科隆（Christoval Colon）。

其撰写其生平的胞子为我们留下了关于其形貌、风度与私人品格的详尽记述；凡此皆温文尔雅且引人入胜。本诗作者藏有他的肖像画。那是一幅真人大小的半身油画像，临摹自佛罗伦萨画廊的原作真迹。

## Preface / 序言
===Original===

In preparing this work for publication it seems proper to offer some observations explanatory of its design. The classical reader will perceive the obstacles which necessarily presented themselves in reconciling the nature of the subject with such a manner of treating it as should appear the most poetical, and at the same time the most likely to arrive at that degree of dignity and usefulness to which it ought to aspire.

*The Columbiad* is a patriotic poem; the subject is national and historical. Thus far it must be interesting to my countrymen. But most of the events were so recent, so important and so well known, as to render them inflexible to the hand of fiction. The poem, therefore, could not with propriety be modelled after that regular epic form which the more splendid works of this kind have taken, and on which their success is supposed in a great measure to depend. The attempt would have been highly injudicious; it must have diminished and debased a series of actions which were really great in themselves and could not be disfigured without losing their interest.

I shall enter into no discussion on the nature of the epopea, nor attempt to prove by any latitude of reasoning that I have written an Epic Poem. The subject indeed is vast; far superior to any one of those on which the celebrated poems of this description have been constructed; and I have no doubt but the form I have given to the work is the best that the subject would admit. It may be added, that in no poem are the unities of time, place and action more rigidly observed: the action, in the technical sense of the word, consisting only of what takes place between Columbus and Hesper; which must be supposed to occupy but few hours, and is confined to the prison and the mount of vision.

But these circumstances of classical regularity are of little consideration in estimating the real merit of any work of this nature. Its merit must depend on the importance of the action, the disposition of the parts, the invention and application of incidents, the propriety of the illustrations, the liveliness and chastity of the images, the suitable intervention of machinery, the moral tendency of the manners, the strength and sublimity of the sentiments; the whole being clothed in language whose energy, harmony and elegance shall constitute a style everywhere suited to the matter they have to treat.

===Chinese===

在付梓本书之际，针对其构思初衷呈献若干阐释性观察似乎甚为合宜。通晓古典诗学的读者自会辨察出在调和题材之天性与最能彰显诗意、且最有望达成其理应企及的尊严与致用程度的笔法之间，必然呈现出的重重阻碍。

《哥伦比亚史诗》是一部爱国诗作；其题材兼具国族性与历史性。就此而言，它对于我的同胞必扣人心弦。然而大多事件因发生未久、至关重大且妇孺皆知，致使其绝难任由虚构之手随意涂抹。因此，本诗不宜依照此类更为辉煌名作所采纳的规整史诗形式来构筑，而那些作品的功成名就常被认为在极大程度上取决于此。作此尝试将是极不智的；它必会削弱并贬损一组本身极为宏伟、一旦横遭扭曲便必丧失其魅力的人类功业。

我不拟深究史诗的本质，亦无意借助任何引申推理来证实自己撰写了一部史诗。这一题材诚然宏伟博大；远迈构筑此类著名诗作所依托的任何单一题材；我深信自己赋予本著作的形式乃是题材所容许的最佳形式。尚可补充的是，在任何诗作中，时间、地点与行动的三一律从未得到过如此严苛的恪守：在诗学严格术语意义上的行动，仅由哥伦布与赫斯珀（Hesper）之间发生的事情所构成；料想不过历时数个时辰，且活动范围局限于牢狱与异象之山。

然而在评估此类著作的真正艺术造诣时，这些古典规整情势其实微不足道。其造诣必须取决于行动之重大、各部排布之精当、情节之创设与铺陈、比兴之妥帖、意象之生动与纯正、神机制宜之契合、风尚之道德旨归，以及情思之雄健与崇高；通篇熔铸于以气势、和谐与典雅构成处处契合所论命题的文风之语言中。

===Original===

It is impossible for me to determine how far I may have succeeded in any of these particulars. This must be decided by others, the result of whose decision I shall never know. But there is one point of view in which I wish the reader to place the character of my work, before he pronounces on its merit: I mean its political tendency. There are two distinct objects to be kept in view in the conduct of a narrative poem; the *poetical* object and the *moral* object. The poetical is the fictitious design of the action; the moral is the real design of the poem.

In the *Iliad* of Homer the poetical object is to kindle, nourish, sustain and allay the anger of Achilles. This end is constantly kept in view; and the action proper to attain it is conducted with wonderful judgment through a long series of incidents, which elevate the mind of the reader, and excite not only a veneration for the creative powers of the poet, but an ardent emulation of his heroes, a desire to imitate and rival some of the great actors in the splendid scene; perhaps to endeavor to carry into real life the fictions with which we are so much enchanted.

Such a high degree of interest excited by the first object above mentioned, the fictitious design of the action, would make it extremely important that the second object, the real design of the poem, should be beneficial to society. But the real design in the *Iliad* was directly the reverse. Its obvious tendency was to inflame the minds of young readers with an enthusiastic ardor for military fame; to inculcate the pernicious doctrine of the divine right of kings; to teach both prince and people that military plunder was the most honorable mode of acquiring property; and that conquest, violence and war were the best employment of nations, the most glorious prerogative of bodily strength and of cultivated mind.

===Chinese===

我无法断言自己在这些细节上究竟取得了多大成功。这必须留待他人定夺，其裁决的结果我亦将无从知晓。但在读者对其艺术造诣作出评判之前，我希望读者从一个特定视角来审视我这部作品的品格：我指的是其政治旨归。在叙事长诗的运思中，必须着眼于两大截然不同的目标：诗学目标与道德目标。诗学目标是行动的虚构构思；道德目标则是全诗的真实主旨。

在荷马的《伊利亚特》中，诗学目标在于点燃、滋养、维系并平息阿喀琉斯的愤怒。这一宗旨被始终如一地铭记在心；而用于达成该目标的得当行动，贯穿一系列令人赞叹的情节构思展开，既升华了读者的心智，又不仅激发了对诗人造化之力的敬仰，更激发起对英雄的热切效仿，渴望摹仿并匹敌这幕壮丽场景中的若干伟大角色；甚或企图将令我们如此陶醉的虚构带入现实生活之中。

由上述首要目标（行动的虚构构思）所激起的如此浓烈的兴味，便使得第二目标（全诗的真实主旨）是否有益于社会变得至关重大。然而《伊利亚特》的真实主旨却恰恰背道而驰。其显见的倾向在于用对尚武名望的狂热激情煽动年轻读者的心智；灌输君权神授的有害学说；教导君王与民众认定武装劫掠乃是获取财产的最光彩手段；并宣扬征服、暴力与杀伐乃是国家最崇高的营生，是强健体魄与开化心智最光荣的特权。

===Original===

How much of the fatal policy of states, and of the miseries and degradations of social man, have been occasioned by the false notions of honor inspired by the works of Homer, it is not easy to ascertain. The probability is, that however astonishing they are as monuments of human intellect, and how long soever they have been the subject of universal praise, they have unhappily done more harm than good. My veneration for his genius is equal to that of his most idolatrous readers; but my reflections on the history of human errors have forced upon me the opinion that his existence has really proved one of the signal misfortunes of mankind.

The moral tendency of the *Aeneid* of Virgil is nearly as pernicious as that of the works of Homer. Its poetical or fictitious design, the settlement of his hero in Italy, is well delineated and steadily pursued. This object must have been far more interesting to the Romans than the anger of Achilles could have been to the Greeks. Had Virgil written his poem one or two centuries earlier than he did, while his countrymen felt that they had a country and were not themselves the property of a master, they must have glowed with enthusiasm in reciting the fabulous labors of their ancestors, and adored the songster who could have thus elevated so endearing a subject; who could have adorned it with such an interesting variety of incidents, such weight of pathos, such majesty of sentiment and harmony of verse. But Virgil wrote and felt like a subject, not like a citizen. The real design of his poem was to increase the veneration of the people for a master, whoever he might be, and to encourage like Homer the great system of military depredation.

===Chinese===

荷马著述所激发的虚妄荣誉观，究竟在多大程度上酿成了列国致命的施政国策，以及社会人类的无尽凄苦与堕落，实难精准考量。极可能发生的情况是：尽管作为人类心智的纪念碑它们令人惊叹不已，且无论它们长久以来赢得了何等普遍的赞誉，令人痛惜的是，它们所造成的戕害远甚于益处。我并非孤立持有此种见解。柏拉图基于同一论据将荷马逐出其设想的共和国；且在所有时代，不仅哲人，凡关注人类道德教化之人皆曾表达过类似的情感。

维吉尔在《埃涅阿斯纪》中采纳了更为温和的原则；但他并未越出古代诗人的惯用范式，将战争描绘为英雄的最崇高事业。

===Original===

Lucan is the only republican among the ancient epic poets. But the action of his rambling though majestic poem is so badly arranged as to destroy, in a poetical sense, the life and interest of the great national subject on which it is founded; at the same time that it abounds in the most exalted sentiments and original views of manners, highly favorable to the love of justice and the detestation of war. If a mind, formed like that of Lucan, as to its moral and political cast, and endowed with the creative energy of Homer, had sung to the early Greeks the fall of Troy or the labors of Hercules, his work (taking the place which those of Homer have unfortunately occupied) as a splendid model for all succeeding ages, would have given a very different turn to the pursuits of heroes and the policy of nations. Ambition might then have become a useful passion, instead of a destructive disease.

In the poem here presented to the public the objects, as in other works of the kind, are two: the fictitious object of the action and the real object of the poem. The first of these is to sooth and satisfy the desponding mind of Columbus; to show him that his labors, though ill rewarded by his cotemporaries, had not been performed in vain; that he had opened the way to the most extensive career of civilization and public happiness; and that he would one day be recognised as the author of the greatest benefits to the human race. This object is steadily kept in view; and the actions, images and sentiments are so disposed as probably to attain the end. But the real object of the poem embraces a larger scope: it is to inculcate the love of rational liberty, and to discountenance the deleterious passion for violence and war; to show that on the basis of the republican principle all good morals, as well as good government and hopes of permanent peace, must be founded; and to convince the student in political science that the theoretical question of the future advancement of human society, till states as well as individuals arrive at universal civilization, is held in dispute and still unsettled only because we have had too little experience of organised liberty in the government of nations to have well considered its effects.

===Chinese===

卢坎是古代史诗诗人中唯一的共和主义者。但他那部虽气势磅礴却松散漫衍的长诗，其行动安排如此拙劣，以至在诗学意义上摧毁了其赖以立足的伟大国族题材的生机与兴味；尽管该诗充溢着极其崇高的情操与关于民风习俗的独创见解，极有利于对公义的热爱与对战火的痛恨。设若有一颗在道德与政治品貌上塑造成如卢坎一般、且兼备荷马那般独创伟力的心智，当年曾向早期希腊人歌咏特洛伊的陷落或赫拉克勒斯的苦难功业，那么其作品（取代荷马作品不幸所占据的地位）作为后继所有世代的光辉典范，必将赋予英雄的追求与万邦的国策以截然不同的导向。届时抱负野心本可成为一股致用有益的激情，而非一场毁灭性的瘟疫痼疾。

在此呈献给公众的本诗中，正如在其他同类著作中一样，目标兼具二者：行动的虚构目标与全诗的真实目标。前者在于安抚并慰藉哥伦布绝望的心智；向他昭示他的劳苦纵使未获同时代人的善待，却绝非徒劳；昭示他已为最为广阔的文明化进程与普世福祉劈开了坦途；且他有朝一日必将被公认为全人类最伟大恩泽的缔造者。这一目标得到了始终如一的恪守；行动、意象与情愫的排布皆旨在达成这一终点。然而全诗的真实目标涵盖了更宏大的格局：它旨在灌输对理性自由的热爱，并抵制对暴力与战火的有害迷狂；阐明在共和原则的基石之上，一切优良道德、良好政制以及长久和平的期许皆必须由此奠定；并向政治学学者确证：人类社会不断前行直至列国与个人皆步入普世文明的这一理论命题，之所以迄今仍悬而未决且饱受争论，唯因我们在国家统治中经历的有组织自由的实践尚浅，未能充分审度其深远效应。

===Original===

I cannot expect that every reader, nor even every republican reader, will join me in opinion with respect to the future progress of society and the civilization of states; but there are two sentiments in which I think all men will agree: that the event is desirable, and that to believe it practicable is one step towards rendering it so. This being the case they ought to pardon a writer, if not applaud him, for endeavoring to inculcate this belief.

I have taken the liberty, notwithstanding the recency of the events, to make some changes in the order of several of the principal battles described in this poem. I have associated the actions of Starke, Herkimer, Brown and Francis in the battle of Saratoga, though they happened at some distance from that battle, both as to time and place. A like circumstance will be noticed with respect to Sumter, Jackson of Georgia and some others in the battle of Eutaw. I have supposed a citadel mined and blown up in the siege of York, and two ships of war grappled and blown up in the naval battle of De Grasse and Graves. It is presumed that these circumstances require no apology; as in the two latter cases the events are incidental to such situations, and they here serve the principal purpose, being meant to increase our natural horror for the havoc and miseries of war in general. And with regard to the two former cases we ought to consider that, in the epic field, the interest to be excited by the action cannot be sustained by following the gazette, as Lucan has done. The desultory parts of the historical action must be brought together and be made to elevate and strengthen each other, so as to press upon the mind with the full force of their symmetry and unity. Where the events are recent and the actors known, the only duty imposed by that circumstance on the poet is to do them historical justice, and not ascribe to one hero the actions of another. But the scales of justice in this case are not necessarily accompanied by the calendar and the map.

===Chinese===

我无法指望每一位读者、甚至无法指望每一位共和主义读者，在关涉社会的未来进步与列国文明化上皆能苟同我的见解；但在两点情愫上我相信人人皆会赞同：这一前景令人神往，且确信其切实可行乃是促成其实现的第一步。既然如此，人们即便不向一位致力于灌输此信念的著述者报以喝彩，亦当宽宥其执着之情。

尽管事件发生未久，我仍擅自对本诗所描绘的数场主要战役的先后次序作了若干变通。在萨拉托加大捷中，我将斯塔克、赫基默、布朗与弗朗西斯的功勋联结在一起，尽管就时间与地点而言，他们所立功勋与该战役皆有一定距离。关于萨姆特、佐治亚的杰克逊等人在尤托之役中的情形亦可见类似变通。在约克镇围城战中，我设想了一处被暗道爆破炸毁的要塞；在德格拉斯与格雷夫斯的海战中，我描摹了两艘纠缠咬合而后轰然炸裂的战舰。料想此类情势无需致歉；因在后两例中，此类变故本属此类战局之常态，且在诗中服务于主要宗旨，旨在激发起我们对整个战火浩劫与惨状的天然惊骇。而关于前两例，我们理当考虑到在史诗领域中，由行动激发的兴味绝难通过如卢坎那样照搬邸报战报来维系。历史行动的零散片段必须聚拢合一，使其相互烘托强化，从而以对称与统一的全部力量震撼读者的心智。在事件发生未久且人物皆为世人所知之处，此情势加诸诗人的唯一职责，乃是给予他们历史的公道，切不可张冠李戴将一位英雄的功劳归于另一人。然而在此情形下，公义的天平未必非得时刻伴随着历书与舆图的刻板拘泥。

===Original===

It will occur to most of my readers that the modern modes of fighting, as likewise the instruments and terms now used in war, are not yet rendered familiar in poetical language. It is doubtless from an unwarrantable timidity, or want of confidence in their own powers of description, that modern poets have made so little use of this kind of riches that lay before them. I confess that I imbibed the common prejudice, and remained a long time in the error of supposing that the ancients had a poetical advantage over us in respect to the dignity of the names of the weapons used in war, if not in their number and variety. And when I published a sketch of the present poem, under the title of *The Vision of Columbus*, I labored under the embarrassment of that idea. I am now convinced that the advantage, at least as to the weapons, is on the side of the moderns. There are better sounding names and more variety in the instruments, works, stratagems and other artifices employed in our war system than in theirs. In short, the modern military dictionary is more copious than the ancient, and the words at least as poetical.

As to the mode of fighting, we have, poetically speaking, lost something in one respect, but we have gained much in another. Our battles indeed admit but few single combats, or trials of individual prowess. They do admit them however; and it is not impossible to describe them with as much detail and interest as the nature of the action requires; as Voltaire has proved in the single combat of Aumale and Turenne in the *Henriad*. Had he managed his general descriptions and the other parts of the conduct of his poem as well, he would have made it a far more interesting work than he has. However, since our single combats must be insignificant in their consequences, not deciding anything as to the result of the battle, it would be inconvenient and misplaced to make much use of them in our descriptions. And here lies our disadvantage, compared with the ancients.

===Chinese===

大多数读者皆会意识到，现代作战方式，以及当今战争中所使用的器械与术语，在诗歌语言中尚未广为通行。现代诗人之所以极少动用眼前这笔财富，无疑是源于一种毫无由来的怯懦，或是对自身描摹伟力缺乏自信。我坦承自己曾深受世俗偏见的浸染，在很长一段时间里陷于误区，设想古人在用于战争的武器名号的尊严上（即便在数量与多样性上并非如此）对我们享有诗学上的优长。而当我以此前的《哥伦布的异象》为题发表本诗初稿时，我曾受困于该构想的羁绊。如今我确信，至少就武器而言，优势在现代人这一边。在我们战争体系中所采用的器械、工程、计谋与其他机巧中，有着更加动听的名号与更为丰富的品类。简而言之，现代军事辞典比古代更为宏富，其措辞至少同样洋溢着诗意。

至于作战方式，从诗意而言，我们在某一维度虽有所失，在另一维度却所获甚丰。现代战役诚然鲜容单挑独斗或个人勇武的较量。然而它们并非全然排斥单打独斗；且以行动性质所要求的那般详实与兴味予以描摹，绝非断无可能；正如伏尔泰在《亨利亚特》中对欧马勒与蒂雷纳的单挑对决所证实的那样。假若他对全局性场面的描摹及全诗的推进布局亦能处理得同等精湛，他本可创作出一部比现状远为引人入胜的力作。然而鉴于我们的单打独斗在后果上必然微不足道、绝难定夺整场战役的胜负，在我们的描摹中大肆动用此类手法将显得不合时宜且错置倒置。这便是我们同古人相比所处的不利之处。

===Original===

But in a general engagement, the shock of modern armies is, beyond comparison, more magnificent, more sonorous and more discoloring to the face of nature, than the ancient could have been; and is consequently susceptible of more pomp and variety of description. Our heaven and earth are not only shaken and tormented with greater noise, but filled and suffocated with fire and smoke. If Homer, with his Grecian tongue and all its dialects, had had the battle of Blenheim to describe, the world would have possessed a picture and a piece of music which now it will never possess. The description would have astonished all ages, and enriched every language into which it might have been translated.

With regard to naval battles the moderns have altogether the advantage. But there has been no naval battle described in modern poetry; neither is there any remaining to us from the ancients, except that in the bay of Marseilles by Lucan, and that near Syracuse by Silius. It would seem strange indeed that Homer, whose wonderful powers of fiction were not embarrassed by historical realities, and who in other respects is so insatiable of variety, did not introduce a sea fight either in the defence of Troy or in the disastrous voyages of Ulysses. But the want of this in Homer’s two poems amounts almost to a proof that in his time the nations had not yet adopted any method of fighting at sea; so that the poet could have no such image in his mind.

===Chinese===

但在大规模兵团交锋中，现代军队的猛烈碰撞同古代相比，无可比拟地更为雄浑壮丽、更为震耳欲聋，且更为彻底地重塑了自然的面貌；因此在描摹的排场与多样性上更具潜力。我们的天地不仅受制于更大的巨响撼动折磨，更充斥并窒息着烈焰与浓烟。设若荷马凭借其希腊语及其所有方言，当年能有布伦海姆战役可供描摹，世界本可拥有一幅画卷与一首乐章，而今日却永难得见。那番描摹必将令万代为之惊叹，并极大丰富其所可能译入的每一种语言。

至于海战，现代人则全然占据优势。但现代诗歌中尚未有关于海战的描摹传世；除卢坎笔下的马赛海湾海战，以及西利乌斯笔下的锡拉库扎近海海战之外，古人亦未曾给我们留下任何此类篇章。令人称奇的是，荷马那非凡的虚构才能未受历史真实的羁绊，且在其他方面如此渴求千变万化，却未曾将一场海战引入特洛伊的防卫战或尤利西斯的厄运远航中。然而在荷马的两部史诗中缺乏海战这一事实，几乎足以证明在他所处的时代万邦尚未采纳海上交锋的任何手段；以至诗人在脑海中绝不可能留存此类图景。

===Original===

The business of war, with all its varieties, makes but a small part of the subject of my poem; it ought therefore to occupy but a small portion of its scenery. This is the reason why I have not been more solicitous to vary and heighten the descriptions of battles and other military operations. I make this observation to satisfy those readers who being accustomed to see a long poem chiefly occupied with this sort of bustle conceive that the life and interest of such compositions depend upon it. How far the majesty or interest of epic song really depends upon the tumultuous conflicts of war I will not decide; but I can assure the reader, so far as my experience goes, that these parts of the work are not the most difficult to write. They are scenes that exhibit those vigorous traits of human character which strike the beholder most forcibly and leave the deepest impression. They delight in violent attitudes; and, painting themselves in the strongest colors on the poet’s fancy, they are easy at any time to recall. He varies them at pleasure, he adorns them readily with incidents, and imparts them with spirit to the reader.

My object is altogether of a moral and political nature. I wish to encourage and strengthen, in the rising generation, a sense of the importance of republican institutions; as being the great foundation of public and private happiness, the necessary aliment of future and permanent ameliorations in the condition of human nature.

This is the moment in America to give such a direction to poetry, painting and the other fine arts, that true and useful ideas of glory may be implanted in the minds of men here, to take place of the false and destructive ones that have degraded the species in other countries; impressions which have become so wrought into their most sacred institutions, that it is there thought impious to detect them and dangerous to root them out, though acknowledged to be false. Woe be to the republican principle and to all the institutions it supports, when once the pernicious doctrine of the holiness of error shall creep into the creed of our schools and distort the intellect of our citizens.

===Chinese===

战火之事及其诸般纷繁变貌，不过构成了我这部诗作题材的微末篇章；因而它理当仅占其布景的极少一部分。这便是我未曾苦心孤诣去变通并拔高战斗与其他军事行动描摹的原由。我作此说明旨在答复那些习惯于看到一部长诗主要充斥着此类喧嚣厮杀、并设想此类作品的生机与兴味皆取决于此的读者。史诗歌咏的庄严或兴味究竟在多大程度上取决于战火的骚动冲突，我无意妄断；但就我的阅历而言，我敢向读者保证，作品的这些篇章绝非最难驾驭之笔。它们是展现人类性格骁勇特质的场景，能给观者以最强烈的震撼并留下最深刻的印记。它们沉醉于激烈的身姿举止；并在诗人脑海中以最鲜明的色彩自我着色，随时皆易于唤起。诗人可随心所欲加以变通，信手点染以各色情节，并神采奕奕地传递给读者。

我的宗旨全然属于道德与政治的范畴。我渴望在年轻一代心中激发起并强化对于共和建制之重要性的自觉；因其乃是公私福祉的伟大基石，是人类境况在未来获得恒久改良所不可或缺的滋养。

此刻正是亚美利加（America）为诗歌、绘画与其他美术赋予如此导向的良机：将关于荣耀的真正致用观念植根于此地民众的心智之中，以取代在其他国度令人类族群蒙受堕落的虚妄与破坏性观念；那些印记在别邦已深植于其最神圣的建制之中，以至揭露它们被视作亵渎神明，根除它们被视作险象环生，纵使明知其虚妄不公亦不敢轻举妄动。一旦谬误之神圣性的有害学说潜入我们学堂的教条并扭曲了公民的心智，共和原则以及它所支撑的一切制度必将大难临头。

===Original===

*The Columbiad*, in its present form, is such as I shall probably leave it to its fate. Whether it be destined to survive its author, is a question that gives me no other concern than what arises from the most pure and ardent desire of doing good to my country. To my country therefore, with every sentiment of veneration and affection I dedicate my labors.

===Chinese===

《哥伦比亚史诗》以其当下的面貌，极可能便是我任其自决命运之作。它是否注定能比其作者更为长久地流传，除源自为祖国效劳的最纯真炽热的心愿外，并未给我带来任何其他挂怀。因此，怀着敬仰与眷恋之至深情愫，我谨将我的全部劳作奉献给我的祖国。
